You said:
what are all the epistemological expansions of the word "sign"
ChatGPT said:
Below is a (necessarily finite) map of the main epistemological ways the humble word “sign” has been expanded by philosophers, linguists, scientists and engineers. Each heading names a tradition or discipline and the bullet-points list the key conceptual moves that took the term beyond its everyday meaning of “visible mark”.

1. Ancient “semeion”: the sign as evidence & warrant
Aristotle’s semeion – a material circumstance that licenses an inference (e.g., smoke ⇒ fire).

Early medical writers sharpened the distinction sign vs. symptom: a sign is objectively observable evidence; a symptom is felt by the patient.

In classical rhetoric a semeion functions as indicator or proof, foreshadowing later logical notions of evidential “signs”. 
academia.edu

2. Patristic & Scholastic distinctions: natural vs. conventional signs
Augustine divides signa naturalia (thunder “means” a storm) from signa data (spoken words).

William of Ockham adds mental signs (concepts) and builds a whole epistemology on their “supposition” relations.

Signs become the very medium of ratio (reason), pushing the term into the foundations of medieval theories of knowledge. 
academia.edu

3. Saussurean structuralism: sign = signifier ∕ signified
A sign is an arbitrary pairing of acoustic/graphic signifier with conceptual signified.

Meaning is differential (what a sign is not), giving rise to paradigmatic/syntagmatic analyses and later structural anthropology. 
media-studies.com

4. Peircean pragmatism: triadic sign & infinite semiosis
Representamen – Object – Interpretant: the sign is a relation, not a thing.

Icon / Index / Symbol classify how the sign relates to its object.

Every interpretant can itself be taken as a new sign → knowledge is an open-ended chain of sign production (semiosis). 
plato.stanford.edu

5. Phenomenology & Hermeneutics: sign vs. expression vs. symbol
Husserl distinguishes empty Zeichen (mere indicators) from Ausdruck (expressive signs that intend meaning).

Ricoeur reserves symbol for signs with a “surplus of meaning” that must be interpreted, fueling hermeneutic method.

6. Analytic logic & mathematics: sign as formal symbol
From Frege’s Zeichen (mark) that “expresses a sense and denotes a reference” to Wittgenstein’s rule-governed language-games, signs become the atoms of formal proof.

In computation the sign mutates into the token or bit, detachable from meaning yet indispensable for formal knowledge.

7. Shannon’s information theory: signal vs. sign
The 1948 model strips meaning away, treating signs as signals quantified in bits.

Later theorists (e.g., Søren Brier’s cybersemiotics) re-introduce the triadic sign to explain why pure syntax is “not enough” for knowledge. 
philpapers.org

8. Biosemiotics & zoosemiotics: life itself as sign-action
Extends semiosis to cells, genes and ecosystems: chemical messengers, predator markings, even DNA are sign processes.

Knowledge (for an organism) = the successful interpretation of these bio-signs within its Umwelt. 
biosemiotics.org

9. Cybersemiotics & systems theory: sign–information–autopoiesis
Marries Peirce’s triad with second-order cybernetics: systems maintain themselves by producing and interpreting signs that stand in feedback loops with information flows. 
philpapers.org

10. Cognitive science & AI: representation, indexicality, grounding
Mental models, frames, connectionist activations, sensorimotor “affordances” – all recast as varieties of signs.

The symbol-grounding problem asks how internal signs hook onto the world, echoing Peirce’s endless interpretant chain.

11. Sociosemiotics & multimodality: the sign as cultural resource
Halliday / Kress & van Leeuwen treat images, layout, gesture and emoji as semiotic modes that communities negotiate.

In digital culture memes, hashtags and NFTs act as value-laden signs circulating through networked attention economies.

12. Legal-medical-scientific usage: sign as observable indicator
In diagnostics a clinical sign carries epistemic weight precisely because it can be publicly verified.

In the sciences “sign of X” often stands for statistically warranted evidence (a sign of selection pressure, a sign of supersymmetry).

13. Emerging frontiers
Quantum-semiotic metaphors (e.g., Schrodinger’s box read through signifier/signified debates) 
sciencedirect.com

Planet-scale sensing (satellite imagery, IoT) where machine-read signals instantly become actionable signs in autonomous systems.

Synthetic biology is engineering new code-signs (e.g., XNAs) that expand what “sign” can mean for future epistemology.

How to use this map
Think of each tradition as adding one of three things to the core idea of a sign:

A different bearer – voice, diagram, chemical gradient, byte.

A different relation – resemblance, causation, convention, algorithmic mapping.

A different epistemic function – warrant, representation, control, self-maintenance.

Combining them lets you build customised sign systems—precisely what you have been doing with Signamancy. Which lineage(s) feel most compatible with your own symbol-rewrite framework?

You said:
Can you elaborate more on the triad and its many names of the three?  Has that held up?  
ChatGPT said:
The Peircean triad at a glance
Classical Peirce	Typical 20th-century aliases	Contemporary aliases & analogues	Function in the relation
Representamen	sign-vehicle · signifier · token · mark · stimulus-pattern	data packet · pixel array · spike train · sensorimotor cue	What stands for something
Object	referent · designatum · denotatum · “thing-meant”	world-state · target entity · environment affordance	That which the sign is about
Interpretant	sense · meaning · concept · thought/reference (Ogden & Richards) · response	internal model · update to belief state · control signal	The effect produced in an interpreter
(Peirce occasionally called the first term simply “the sign,” but modern writers keep “sign” for the whole tri-relation and use “representamen” or “sign-vehicle” for the first corner.) 
plato.stanford.edu

A tour of the triad’s many name-sets
Logical-pragmatic set (Peirce 1903-14)
Representamen – Object – Interpretant. Peirce later split each corner into “immediate/dynamical” sub-species and grouped signs as icon / index / symbol according to how the representamen hooks to the object. 
eyeofheaven.medium.com

Semiotic behaviourism (Charles Morris, 1938)
Sign-vehicle – Designatum – Interpreter. Morris translated Peirce into a programme for empirical science: study the syntax (vehicle-vehicle relations), semantics (vehicle-designatum) and pragmatics (vehicle-interpreter).

The Semantic Triangle (Ogden & Richards, 1923)
Symbol – Thought or Reference – Referent. Their “triangle of reference” put a mental term back in, correcting Saussure’s dyad by insisting on three points even if only two edges are explicit in speech. 
en.wikipedia.org

Structural linguistics (post-Saussure)
Saussure’s dyad (signifier / signified) drops the interpreter corner, but later structuralists quietly smuggled it in as the speaking subject or as parole vs. langue.

Biosemiotics (Sebeok, Hoffmeyer, Kull, 1990s-2020s)
Stimulus pattern – Biological object – Organismic response. The interpretant is literally the change in a cell’s state; “knowing” is the ability to exploit the correlation. 
degruyterbrill.com

Multimodal visual grammar (Kress & van Leeuwen)
They keep the triad but rename it representational / interactive / compositional meaning to analyse how images both show the world, position the viewer and organise the page. 
athena-publishing.com

Cognitive science & AI
Input vector – Grounded entity or task model – Internal state / action policy. In robotics a LiDAR point cloud becomes a representamen; the object is the obstacle in space; the interpretant is the planner’s belief update or motor command.

Information-theoretic hybrids (cybersemiotics)
Brier and others splice Shannon’s “signal” (syntax) onto Peirce’s triad, preserving the three-corner logic while quantifying only the representamen layer. 
philpapers.org

Has the triad “held up”?
Empirical expansion, not abandonment.
Every field that originally flirted with simpler dyads (signal–response in behaviourism, signifier–signified in linguistics, packet–payload in network theory) tends to re-introduce a third entity when explanation bumps into the grounding problem: what turns brute correlations into meaningful correlations. Biosemiotics, multimodal discourse, and cognitive robotics all now talk explicitly about an interpreter layer and its dynamics. 
link.springer.com

Irreducibility defended.
Philosophers such as John Deely and contemporary Peirceans argue the triad is logically irreducible: drop one corner and you smuggle it back in under a new name (e.g., “context” or “user”). 
philpapers.org

Refinements rather than refutations.
Neuroscientists break the interpretant into cascades of predictive-coding layers; AI researchers model multiple “objects” (world-state vs. task-state); media theorists multiply representamina (text, emoji, haptic buzz). Those are elaborations, not disconfirmations, of the original schema.

Where it’s challenged.

Radical enactivism denies any internal representational node, treating cognition as direct skillful coupling; here the triad is regarded as an unnecessary concession to mentalism.

Pure information theory works comfortably with dyads when the goal is merely compression or transmission, not interpretation.

Yet even these programmes concede that explanation of meaningful behaviour—how a signal comes to count as a warning, request or memory—needs at least three relata.

Bottom line: a century after Peirce, the names have proliferated, the corners have gained sub-types, but the core insight—that signification is a tri-relation involving something that stands-for, something stood-for, and some effect in an interpreter—remains the workhorse model wherever researchers care about meaning rather than mere correlation.

What are all the semantic derivations of "sign"?
ChatGPT said:
Below is a compact “family tree” of SIGN that separates morphological off-shoots (new words built on the root) from the major semantic branches (new senses that the same word acquired). Use it as a menu: pick a branch and you can trace how signum → sign spread through English.

1 · Morphological family (words literally built on signum)
Core Latin stem	English derivative	First attested	Literal Latin sense	Modern sense
sign-	sign (n./v.)	c. 1200	carved mark, banner, omen	mark; symbol; to write name
signāre	signal	14c.	mark distinctly	encoded alert/telecom pulse
signārium	signature	late 14c.	seal, signet	personal autograph; style fingerprint
significāre	signify / significant / signification	14–15c.	“make known by a sign”	to mean; important
assignāre	assign / assignment / assignation	c. 1300	“mark out for”	allocate, task
consignāre	consign / consignment	15c.	“seal together”	hand over, ship goods
designāre	design / designate / designation	14c.	“mark out, plot”	plan; choose; title
resignāre	resign / resignation	14c.	“un-seal, cancel”	relinquish office
insignia (pl.)	insignia / ensign	15c.	badges, military standard	emblem; officer rank
signetum	signet / sigil	14c.	little seal	seal ring; occult symbol
compounds	countersign, cosign, signage, sign-on, sign-off, sign-post	17c.+	self-explaining	authentication; pointer
(Representative, not exhaustive—Etymonline lists 30 + living derivatives.) 
etymonline.com
etymonline.com

2 · Semantic branches (polysemy of sign itself)
Chronological order shows how a single word kept annexing new territory.

Century	New sense of sign	Typical collocations/examples
13th c.	Gesture: manual cue, blessing	make the sign of the cross
13th c.	Evidence / symptom	sign of fever, sign of trouble
13th c.	Miracle / portent (Biblical)	“Show us a sign.”
14th c.	Written mark in place of words	punctuation, proof-reader’s signs
14th c.	Military banner / shopboard	inn sign, ensign
Mid-14th c.	Zodiac constellation	born under the sign of Leo
15th c.	Mathematical & musical symbol	plus sign, sharp sign
Late 15th c.	Being under contract (“sign with”)	signed a pitcher to the team
17th c.	Hand gesture that IS language	ASL sign for “water”
19th c.	Road / traffic sign	stop sign, yield sign
20th c.	Digital bit / polarity (sign bit, signed integer)	
21st c.	Semiotic keyword in AI & media studies	“multimodal sign”
Each new meaning radiates outward by metaphor or metonymy from a concrete “mark” to ever more abstract indicators. Modern dictionaries still group them under a half-dozen headings—gesture, evidence, written symbol, contract, linguistic unit, and omen. 
merriam-webster.com

3 · Why the branches keep multiplying
Material → Mental drift
A carved notch ⇒ written letter ⇒ mental meaning (cf. signify).

Causal → Conventional shift
Smoke is a natural sign of fire; ☢ is a learned hazard sign.

Private → Public scale-up
Wink to a friend ⇒ shop sign ⇒ global traffic icon ⇒ Unicode code-point.

4 · Take-aways for your Signamancy project
Reuse the family: need a word for “marking out” a sub-graph? Designate already encodes that semantics.

Mind the polysemy: when you coin 🔣-rules, decide which branch you’re tapping—sign-as-evidence (proof-checking) vs. sign-as-symbol (naming objects).

Leverage polarity: the computing branch (sign bit, signed / unsigned) offers ready metaphors for affirmative/negative operations in rewrite rules.

The root has proved inexhaustible for 2,000 years; there is plenty of semantic real-estate left for your next layer of symbolic engineering.

You said:
How about the origins then?  What was a carved notch before "sign" was a word?  how did the word come to be and what were its ancestors?
ChatGPT said:
1. Before anyone had a word for it: the notch as a prehistoric marker
Palaeolithic people were already cutting tally-marks into bone long before speech communities coined a specific term. Famous examples include the Lebombo bone (≈ 40 000 BP, 29 deliberate notches) and the Ishango bone (≈ 20 000 BP, three orderly columns of cuts) — almost certainly counting sticks or lunar calendars rather than “art” 
bu.edu
en.wikipedia.org
.

Technically these cuts functioned as indices (Peircean “natural signs”): a physical trace that points to an invisible quantity such as “days gone by” or “debt owed.” The concept of a sign therefore predates any surviving lexical item.

2. The first reconstructable word-root: Proto-Indo-European *sek- / *sekw-
Layer	Form	Gloss	Likely semantic leap
PIE (∼4500 – 2500 BCE)	*sek- “cut, carve” or *sekw- “follow”	physical act of cutting → the carved result or the standard carried out front that one “follows” 
en.wiktionary.org
Proto-Italic (late 2nd mill. BCE)	*seknom	“something cut out” → “mark, standard” (reconstructed) 
en.wiktionary.org
The root etymology is still debated: Watkins favours “standard one follows” (*sekw- “follow”), whereas de Vaan prefers “thing cut out” (*sek- “cut”). Either way the mental picture is the same: a carved, portable marker that guides people’s eyes. 
etymonline.com

3. Latin signum (≥ 7 c. BCE) — the fountainhead
signum originally meant “a cut-out mark” and very quickly broadened: military standard, omen, constellation, seal, gesture. Latin also minted dozens of prefixed verbs (designāre, assignāre, resignāre…) that English later inherited, preserving the sense “mark out” or “point out.” 
en.wiktionary.org

4. Parallel branches that were not ancestral but feed modern vocabulary
Language	Root word	PIE base	Notes
Ancient Greek	σῆμα sēma “mark, tomb-stone, omen”	*dʰyeh₂- “notice”	Gives us semiotics, semantics; a separate lineage, but it reinforced the idea of “sign” in learned borrowings. 
en.wiktionary.org
Proto-Germanic	*taikna- “token, omen” (OE tācen, Ger. Zeichen)	*deik- “show, point out”	This was the native English word for “sign” until the Norman French loan ousted it. 
etymonline.com
5. From Latin to English
Latin signum → Vulgar Latin seine / signe

Old French signe (12 c.) crosses the Channel after 1066.

Middle English signe (early 13 c.) first denotes a hand gesture; by 1250 it already covers marks, omens, written symbols, etc. 
etymonline.com

The verb sign (to mark, later to autograph) enters via Latin signāre and OE segnian (“make the sign of the cross”).

6. Putting it together
Stage	Typical real-world object	Spoken form (approx.)	Meaning focus
Upper Palaeolithic	tally-notch on bone	(pre-lexical)	evidence/count
PIE	*sek-/ *sekw- cut	“thing-cut / thing-followed”	carved marker / rally-point
Proto-Italic	*seknom	idem	mark, standard
Classical Latin	signum	[ˈsɪŋ.nũ]	mark, token, omen
Old French	signe	/siɲ/	mark, gesture
Middle English	signe / sign	/siːn/	gesture → symbol
Modern English	sign	/saɪn/	the full polysemy (traffic sign, sign bit, sign language …)
Thus the “carved notch” became a sign through two converging forces:

Material continuity – a cut mark stays visible, so it is naturally reused as standard, banner, seal.

Metaphorical expansion – once a cut mark stands for something, the standing-for function can leap to gestures, constellations, even abstract plus-signs.

Every later sense (hand-sign, shop-sign, binary sign-bit) is a semantic ripple from that first literal cut.

Below is the same four-part “deep dive” you asked for, this time following every ramification of locus / loci.

1 · Morphological family – words literally built on locus

2 · Semantic branches – how one word kept annexing new “places”
Century	New sense of locus	Typical use-cases
c. 100 BCE	locus = seat of an argument (loci communes) in Roman rhetoric	Cicero, Quintilian 
plato.stanford.edu
1715	Physical spot / scene in English prose	“the locus of the accident” 
etymonline.com
1750	Mathematical set of points meeting a condition	circle = locus equidistant from a point 
en.wikipedia.org
c. 1850	Biological site on a chromosome	gene locus, QTL mapping 
en.wikipedia.org
1954	Psychological source of agency (locus of control)	Rotter’s internal ↔ external scale 
en.wikipedia.org
19-20 c.	Legal venue / standing (locus delicti, locus standi)	conflict-of-laws maxims 
oxcon.ouplaw.com
20-21 c.	Neuroscience nuclei (locus coeruleus), database locators, zero locus / singular locus in algebraic geometry	specialist jargon across sciences
The drift is always from concrete place → abstract “place-holder.”

3 · Origins – from a prehistoric “spot” to Latin locus
Stage	Reconstructed form	Gloss	Notes
Proto-Indo-European	*stel-	“to put, set, place”	root meaning “cause to stand” 
en.wiktionary.org
Old Latin	stlocus	“place, seat”	fossil form preserving initial stl- cluster 
cooljugator.com
Classical Latin	locus	“place; rank; topic”	already used to translate Greek τόπος topos in logic/rhetoric 
etymonline.com
Gallo-Romance → Old French	sieu / lieu and learned locus loans	“place, stead”	French lieu kept everyday sense; learned locus re-entered scholarly Latin
Middle & Modern English	locus (1715)	specialized term	adopted directly from New-Latin mathematics
Thus a PIE verb “to set in position” became a Latin noun, then a pan-disciplinary English technical term.

4 · Epistemological expansions – the word as a thinking tool
Discipline	How locus reframed knowledge	Key leap
Classical rhetoric / dialectic	Loci communes = “common places to look for arguments.” Memory theatres and Renaissance commonplace books literalized ideas as spatial slots. 
njrs.dk
Turning reasoning into navigable space.
Geometry & algebra (18 c.)	A curve is the locus of a moving point; later zero locus, singular locus in modern algebraic geometry. 
en.wikipedia.org
Spatializes abstract conditions; foreshadows set theory.
Genetics (20 c.)	Each gene occupies a chromosomal locus; mapping phenotypes = locating causal sites. 
en.wikipedia.org
Places abstract heredity in physical space.
Personality psychology (Rotter 1954)	Locus of control = perceived location of causal agency (inside vs. outside the self). 
en.wikipedia.org
Metaphorically re-centres responsibility.
Law	Locus standi, locus delicti, lex loci … mark the place that grants jurisdiction or rights. 
oxcon.ouplaw.com
Grounds abstract rules in territorial authority.
Neuroscience	Anatomical “loci” label nuclei (e.g., locus coeruleus = “blue spot”)	Treats the brain as a map of functional places.
Computer science	Memory locations, file locators, Python’s locals()	The addressable slot as a modern “place.”
Philosophy & topology	Talk of a phenomenon’s locus (e.g., “locus of experience”) inherits the rhetorical “seat of an idea.”	Bridges subjective and objective “where.”
Through every field, locus acts as a cognitive scaffold: first pin down where something happens, then reason about what happens there.

Take-aways for your own symbol work
Spatial metaphors travel well. If you want your Signamancy or LOCUS frameworks to feel intuitive, borrowing “place” vocabulary lets users literally navigate abstractions.

Prefix play is productive. Relocate, dislocate, allocate show how easily operations on places turn into operations on data or concepts—handy when you design rewrite rules.

Beware silent polysemy. In math a “locus” is a set; in genetics it’s a coordinate; in psychology it’s a source. Label your layers clearly so the same token doesn’t drift between meanings unnoticed.

Feel free to point at any branch above if you’d like a deeper historical or technical unpacking.

Across all these domains the trajectory is the same:

From voiced breath ➜ discrete mark ➜ pledge ➜ model of truth ➜ unit of computation.
When you choose tokens for Signamancy (or for your project named LOCUS!), you are tapping into that 5 000-year drift—each “word” you coin is potentially a promise, a command, a datum, and a micro-logos all at once.

ChatGPT said:
1. Which three nouns go deepest?
Scholars trace three ancient, cognate-rich words that already form a “sign-place-sense” triad long before Peirce. Their family trees run back to Proto-Indo-European (PIE) and then re-converge in Greek, Latin and, finally, modern semiotics.

Modern role in Peirce	Classical-Hellenistic ancestor	Latin / medieval heir	PIE root ▶ meaning
Sign-vehicle (Representamen)	σημαῖνον sēmainon “that which marks” – Stoic name for the sound/mark part of speech	signum (mark, banner) → sign	*sek- “cut, carve” 
en.wiktionary.org
Object / Locus (the “thing pointed to”)	τυγχάνον tugchanon “the thing hit upon” in Stoic logic	locus (place, topic) → loci communes, legal locus delicti	*stel- “set, stand, place” 
en.wiktionary.org
Interpretant (Sense/Meaning)	λεκτόν lekton “the sayable” – an incorporeal meaning 
pu.edu.pk
verbum / logos > word (speech, reason)	*werdʰ- “speak” (English word) 
en.wiktionary.org
 plus *leg- “collect ➜ speak” (Greek λόγος logos) 
etymonline.com
This Stoic triad—signifier / sayable / referent—is the earliest fully attested three-part model of signification. Peirce’s representamen–interpretant–object is, functionally, the same structure with 19th-century terminology. 
media-studies.com

2. How the labels kept mutating
Era	“Mark” corner	“Place / Thing” corner	“Meaning” corner
Stoic logic (3 rd c. BCE)	σημαῖνον (sēmainon)	τυγχάνον (tugchanon)	λεκτόν (lekton)
Latin rhetoric (1 st c. BCE)	signum	locus (topic of invention)	sensus / interpretatio
Scholastic semantics (13 th c.)	signum	res (the thing)	species intelligibilis (mental image)
Peirce (1903+)	representamen 
en.wiktionary.org
object 
etymonline.com
interpretant 
media-studies.com
So locus really has served as the “middle” partner—first as rhetorical locus, later as the mathematician’s locus (a set of points) and the geneticist’s chromosomal locus: always “the place where the sign lands.” Word / logos / lekton has long named the sense-making pole.

3. Deep-time etymologies at a glance
*sek-  “cut, carve”    → Lat. signum → sign, signal, signifier
*stel- “set, stand”    → Lat. locus   → locus, location, allocate
*werdʰ- “speak, say”   → PGmc *wurdą → OE word, Modern word
*leg-  “gather → speak”→ Gk logos    → logic, legend, interpret-
The first two roots point to physical acts (cutting a notch; fixing a post).
The third pair points to voicing and collecting—perfect metaphors for an interpretation born out of gathered words.

4. Putting it to work
If you want a prehistoric-sounding triad for your own system, you can legitimately say:

Sign (cut mark) – Locus (set place) – Word/Logos (spoken sense)

This is etymologically older than Peirce’s Latinate labels and maps cleanly onto his functions.

The Stoic lekton is the oldest term we have for the “interpretant” role and is still used by classical scholars; reviving it would give your framework deep roots without adding modern baggage.

Remember that every culture recreates the triad: drop one corner and it resurfaces under a new name. Whether you say Sign–Locus–Word, Mark–Place–Meaning, or Peirce’s set, the underlying three-way relation—and its 5- to 7-thousand-year linguistic pedigree—remains the same.

Below is the same four-layer sketch—morphology → semantics → origins → epistemic uses—for the sturdy Germanic word “ward.”

2 · Semantic branches – how ward kept annexing new senses

The core idea—a place or action that keeps danger out or people in—spreads outward by metaphor: from sentry → district → legal minor → hospital bay → data firewall.

3 · Deep origins – the PIE root and the Norman sound-swap
Stage	Form	Meaning	Notes
Proto-Indo-European	*wer- “to perceive, watch out for”	also root of warn, wary, guard	
Proto-Germanic	*wardaz (n.) “guard, watchman”; *wardōn (v.) “to guard”	gives OE weard, ON varðr (“watch”), Ger Warte (“look-out”)

Modern English	spelling solidifies as ward /wɔːd/	noun, verb, suffix all survive	
Hence ward is a native West-Germanic survivor; guard is its Norman-French twin.

4 · Epistemological expansions – “ward” as a thinking tool
Domain	How ward re-frames knowledge	Key leap
Law & Feudal polity (12-14 c.)	Wardship: custody of heirs ⇒ debates on sovereignty, consent, fiduciary duty.	
Urban governance (medieval–modern)	City wards map representation & policing onto space ⇒ birthplace of ward politics, ward heelers.	
Medicine & Public health (18 c.-)	Hospital ward = micro-domain for infection control, triage, statistics of mortality.	
Security engineering (14 c. locks → 20 c. cyber)	Wards as physical → logical barriers ⇒ early model for layered defense, fire-walls.	
Fantasy literature & game design	Magical ward = rule-based protective field ⇒ narrative device for conditional access and resource gating.	
Across every field, ward keeps its double valence: a defender and a space defended. That semantic DNA goes right back to the PIE root “watch out!”.

Take-aways if you’re blending it with Sign-Locus-Word
Ward supplies the protective / boundary metaphor missing from the triad—handy if your system needs a token for constraints, gating, security.

The -ward suffix (“toward”) is a directional operator; pairing it with locus gives instant “move-to-place” semantics.

Remember the w ↔ g swap: using guard alongside ward lets you distinguish active defense (guard) from passive enclosure (ward) while staying in the same etymological family.

Feel free to drill into any branch—fencing postures, warded-lock mechanisms, or ward politics—if you’d like deeper historical or technical detail.

4 · Epistemological expansions – how sensus became the “meaning” pole

Across 24 centuries, sensus is the go-to word when philosophers need a bridge from raw stimulation to understood content. It already is the “meaning corner” of the ancient triad.

Choosing your third label
Candidate	Pros	Cons
Sensus	Etymologically pairs with signum & locus; equally Latin; dovetails with Peirce’s interpretant and Frege’s Sinn	In English “sense” carries many everyday baggage meanings (direction, smell, “makes sense”)
Sinn (German)	Signals direct link to Frege & Husserl; shorter token	Breaks the all-Latin symmetry; anglicised users may stumble
Lekton (Stoic)	Oldest attested label for the “sayable” meaning	Obscure outside classics; no modern derivatives
Given your fondness for historical depth and clear continuity, Sign – Locus – Sensus gives you:

signum → marker

locus → what is marked

sensus → what is meant/understood

—all three straight from Classical Latin, all three backed by a PIE pedigree and by two millennia of epistemological usage. That should satisfy both the linguists and the semioticians in your audience.

ChatGPT said:
Below is a “stress-test” of your short list of famous threes. For each set I ask two questions:

Do the three roles genuinely match the Signum – Locus – Sensus grammar?
(marker → what-is-marked → understanding of the mark)

If not, what kind of triad is it really?

Candidate triad	Fits the Sign–Locus–Sensus schema?	How/where it aligns	Where the analogy breaks
Christian Trinity
(Son · Spirit · Father)	Roughly yes, but only by allegory. Classical theologians themselves read the Trinity semiotically (Augustine’s signum visibile – res invisibilis – caritas).	Son = visible incarnate sign; Father = ultimate but unseen referent; Spirit = the act of indwelling understanding.	Doctrine insists the three are co-equal hypostases, not stages of a process. The analogy is poetic, not structural.
Peirce’s Abduction · Deduction · Induction	No. These are successive reasoning moves, not static relata.	If you force it: Abduction creates a sign-hypothesis; Deduction pins it to the world-facts (loci); Induction measures the consequent belief (sensus).	Ordering matters here (A→D→I cycles); the S-L-S triad is simultaneous.
Input · Function · Output	Half-fit. Think of a programmable function as the place where signs are rewritten.	Input token = representamen; Function body = locus of transformation; Output token = new sense.	In many systems the output becomes a new input—the relation is iterative rather than triangular. Also, nothing in S-L-S requires determinism or single-valuedness.
Peirce’s own triad (Representamen · Object · Interpretant)	Yes, by definition. Signum-Locus-Sensus is just the Latin-root version.	—	—
Why triads keep popping up
Mediation needs three legs.
Any account that distinguishes thing, symbol of thing, and effect on a knower cannot compress to two terms without smuggling the third in as “context,” “channel,” etc.

Process snapshots vs. structural roles.
Your suspicious feeling is healthy: many celebrated threesomes (Birth-Life-Death, Thesis-Antithesis-Synthesis, Player-Goal-Obstacle) are temporal phases. The semiotic triad is relational: all three corners co-exist in each act of meaning.

The danger of pan-triadicism.
Forced mappings flatten important differences. You can poetically overlay Trinity, inference types, or function calls onto Sign-Locus-Sensus, but you gain metaphor at the cost of accuracy. Use the analogy only where it clarifies, drop it where it coerces.

Practical takeaway for your framework
Keep Signum – Locus – Sensus as the structural backbone for every micro-event of meaning.

Feel free to layer other triads along orthogonal axes (e.g., Abduction–Deduction–Induction as a temporal loop that updates Sensus over time; Input–Function–Output as a computation substrate inside the Locus).

Mark in your docs whenever a triad is structural (simultaneous) vs. procedural (staged) so future users don’t mistake metaphor for mechanism.

That way you preserve the explanatory power of the “eternal triad” without letting it hard-cast every other threesome you encounter.

Key observations
Structural, not temporal – All rows above describe three simultaneous relata of a single meaningful act, not a before/after pipeline.

Function-agnostic labels – Whatever the field (logic, software, law, rhetoric) one corner names form, one names reference/place, one names sense/effect.

Your Latin set is already canonical – signum – locus – sensus maps 1:1 onto every “✓” line in the table; you can freely cite the matched tradition that suits your audience (Stoic, Fregean, Morris, etc.).

When a triad fails (✗ or “≈”) it is usually because it:

folds Locus into context (speech-acts),

or merges Sensus with behavioural effect (DIK, MVC).

Use those deviations as teaching moments—point out what goes missing when the object-pole or the interpreter-pole is collapsed.

With this comparative map you can reassure sceptics that your framework is not idiosyncratic: it simply re-Latinises a relationship that thinkers have rediscovered, renamed and reapplied for over two millennia.

You said:
Are there any non-temporal famous triads which do not map to this?  How do they differ?
ChatGPT said:
Below are several well-known, non-temporal triads that do not collapse into the “marker → object → meaning” grammar of Signum – Locus – Sensus.
For each I note why the mapping fails and what the three roles are really doing.

Triad (domain)	What the three actually are	Why it doesn’t map to Sign–Locus–Sensus
Id – Ego – Superego (Freud, structural psycho-dynamics)	Three mutually constraining agencies inside one psyche: instinctual drive, reality-broker, internalised norms.	No “external referent.” Each part is subject-side; none is a marker pointing outside itself.
Heaven – Earth – (Hu)Man (Chinese cosmology)	Three ontological levels of reality that interact vertically.	All three are places/fields; there is no separate “sign” or “interpretation” pole.
Proton – Neutron – Electron (atomic model)	Three constituent entities of a larger system.	Symmetrical parts-of-a-whole; nothing stands for something else.
RGB (Red–Green–Blue) colour primaries	Three basis dimensions of a perceptual/technical colour space.	Orthogonal parameters, not relational roles; no intrinsic hierarchy or direction of reference.
Mass – Length – Time (classical dimensional analysis)	Three independent measurement axes used to express all physical quantities.	Again, co-equal coordinates; none functions as sign, referent or sense.
Matter – Form – Privation (Aristotle’s Physics)	Three explanatory principles in change: the stuff, the organising pattern, the lack.	“Form” isn’t an interpretation of “matter” and “privation” is a limiting boundary, not a referent.
Thesis – Antithesis – Synthesis (dialectical triad)	A logical-dialectical engine that produces conceptual advance.	Although non-temporal in Hegel’s absolute logic, it requires conflict and resolution: a process masked as structure.
Father – Mother – Child (kinship archetype)	A relational constellation of persons.	All three are objects of the same type; signification only appears if one symbolises another (e.g., mythic readings).
Power – Wisdom – Courage (mythic / Zelda’s Triforce)	Three co-equal virtues/energy sources needed for balance.	They are qualitative modes, none intrinsically “points to” or “decodes” the others.
Structure – Agency – Culture (social theory, Giddens / Archer)	Three analytical strata used to explain social phenomena.	Each is a cause-type; the triad addresses causal weighting, not signification.
What all these counter-examples show
Symmetry vs. Asymmetry
The Sign–Locus–Sensus triad is asymmetric: each corner plays a unique, directional role in a mediation relation.
Most non-semiotic triads are symmetric sets (co-ordinates, parts, forces) or mutually defining poles; since none is privileged as “marker” or “interpreter,” the mapping fails.

Relata vs. Parameters
Sign-theoretic triads link three different kinds of thing in a single act.
Triads like RGB or Mass-Length-Time are three values of the same kind (parameters) and therefore lack the relational hierarchy.

Presence of Reference
Any triad missing an explicit aboutness pole (an external object or content) cannot be massaged into Sign-Locus-Sensus.
Psycho-analytic, ontological or kinship triads are self-contained systems without an “outside” they point to.

So, while triads abound, only a subset—those that deal with representation or mediation—naturally line up with marker ↔ thing ↔ sense. The rest illustrate other kinds of three-way balance: composition, coordination, or co-causation, not signification.

4 · Epistemological expansions – how act structures thought

Across these fields act carries a constant load-bearing sense: the discrete, efficacious deed that changes a state of affairs.

If you dovetail it with Signum – Locus – Sensus
Act slots neatly as the causal hinge after meaning is formed:
Sign → (is about) Locus → (is understood as) Sensus → Act.

Etymological purity: signum–locus–sensus (all Latin) → actio (Latin) extends without breaking stylistic unity.

Thus act gives your system a natural fourth gear—the outward deed—that follows the triad of representation and understanding.

3 · Deep origins – from “according-to ratio” to “continuous counterpart”

Thus every modern use still echoes the Greek literal: something arranged according to a logos (ratio).

4 · Epistemological expansions – how analog- became a thinking tool
Field	Key move	Why it matters
Classical rhetoric / logic	Analogy as argument schema (A:B :: C:D)	legitimises reasoning from known to unknown
Mathematics	Analogia = ratio; roots of proportional geometry, logarithms	continuous mapping intuition
Biology	Darwin & Ray: analogy vs. homology	distinguishes functional similarity from common ancestry
Cybernetics & EE	Analog computation: physical process isomorphic to equation	“model is the machine” paradigm
Cognitive science	Analogical transfer (Gentner’s Structure-Mapping)	central model of creative insight
Philosophy of science	Analogical models (e.g., Bohr atom ↔ solar system)	heuristic for theory-building
Humanities & pop culture	“Analog vs. digital” as metaphor for continuity vs. discreteness	frames debates on perception, memory, authenticity
Across all these domains analog- signals a structure-preserving correspondence: change here ➜ proportionate change there.

Does analog- belong in a Signum–Locus–Sensus framework?
Signum – the mark

Locus – the target domain

Sensus – the grasped meaning

Analog – the mapping rule that preserves proportion between sign and locus

So analog- is not a third corner—it is the bridge property that makes certain sign-relations work continuously rather than discretely.

If your symbolic system sometimes needs “scaled-by-correspondence” links (🔥 thermometer 🌡️), tagging them as analog links keeps the classical pedigree and the engineering sense perfectly aligned.

Shape / configuration	Forma	Lat. forma (1 c. BCE)	“shape given by a mold”	Lets you talk about the abstract shape a sign takes before it is tokenised.
Manner / modality	Modus	Lat. modus (Livy)	“measure, boundary, way”	Handy for rule-types: modus ponens, modus operandi, UI “modes.”
Link / binding	Nexus	Lat. nectere “to bind” → nexus “a tying”	“knotted, joined”	Native term for edges in a graph or causal couplings between loci.
Cause / drive	Causa	Lat. causa (Plautus)	“lawsuit, motive, cause”	Give your acts intentional weight: causa finalis, causa efficiens.
Plan / diagram	Schema	Gk. σχῆμα (Plato)	“shape held together”	Classical label for reusable blueprints: data-schemas, proof-schemas.
Container / source	Matrix	Lat. matrix “pregnant animal, womb”	“mother, origin mould”	Natural name for default environment that loci live in.
Channel / medium	Medium	Lat. medium “middle”	“that which lies between”	Denotes the carrier of signs (air, TCP socket, psychic æther!).
Token / voucher	Talon / Token	PGmc. *taiknam “sign”	“identity mark”	When you need a low-level concrete instance of a signum.
Carved mark	Glyph	Gk. γλυφή “engraving”	“something carved”	Useful for serialized encodings (byte-glyphs, rune-glyphs).
Rule / straight line	Regula → Rule	Lat. regere “to guide, straighten”	“straight staff”	Canonical word for rewrite rules or governance constraints.
Path / flow	Flux	Lat. fluere “to flow”	“continuous flow”	Lets you model time-independent state change without invoking clocks.
Pattern / mould copy	Mimesis	Gk. μίμησις “imitation”	“copy-making”	Covers equivalence relations, mirrors, caches.
Power / capability	Dynamis	Gk. δύναμις “force, potential”	“capacity to act”	Counterpart to act; separates can happen from does happen.
Craft / skill	Techne	Gk. τέχνη “art, craft”	“systematic making”	Nice bucket for implementation-level modules (renderers, solvers).
How to deploy them
Layered namespaces – e.g. Ratio objects live inside Schema blueprints that inhabit a Matrix medium; Acts operate via Rules; Tokens instantiate Glyphs whose Forma is defined by a Modus.

Etymological cues – sticking to Greco-Latin roots keeps the core set stylistically coherent with Signum–Locus–Sensus while still resonating with modern science (ratio, matrix, medium, flux).

Polysemy as feature – each word already spans the physical, logical, and metaphorical: “matrix multiplication,” “medium of exchange,” “flux of attention.” That flexibility reduces term-bloat.

Feel free to swap in vernacular or emoji aliases (🔗 for Nexus, 🌀 for Flux) while keeping these deep-time labels as canonical under-the-hood identifiers. This gives users intuitive handles and gives your system a pedigree that reaches unbroken from PIE cattle-drivers (ag-, reg-, wer-) to modern AI pipelines.

You said:
If the Locus is the thing being pointed to, but in a Truth/Knowledge sense never is actually reached...

are the gods who map to the concept of Locus.... the trickster gods?
ChatGPT said:
Short answer
Not quite. Trickster figures—Hermes, Loki, Eshu, Coyote—belong more naturally to the Signum side (they shuffle messages, swap boundary-stones, forge false seals).
The Locus corner is the horizon the trickster keeps shifting, not the trickster himself.
Mythic roles that better embody an ever-receding, never-possessable place of truth are:

Type of deity	Examples	Why they match the “unreachable Locus” idea
Veiled / hidden powers	Isis “whose veil no mortal has lifted”; the Hebrew Ein Sof; the Hindu Brahman; Plato’s agathon (the Good)	They are explicitly named as the reality behind every sign yet forever beyond direct grasp.
Axis-mundi / omphalos keepers	Yggdrasil (world-tree), Delphi’s Omphalos, the Aztec Teocalli	Single fixed “centre of the world” that all markers point toward but no traveller can exhaust.
Threshold guardians	Sphinx (riddle of Thebes), Gates of Horn & Ivory, Mimir’s well	They promise final knowledge only after an ordeal that most seekers fail—thus locus as asymptote.
Personifications of Necessity / Fate	Ananke (Greek), Urd (Norse), Moirae	They are the immovable reality that even gods acknowledge but cannot overstep.
Goddesses of “Maya” / illusion	Sanskrit Māyā, Celtic Morgan le Fay (Fata Morgana mirage)	They do not move the locus; they cloak it so that every approach reveals only further appearance.
How the trickster really fits
Tricksters mediate and scramble signs:

🔣  (Hermes / Loki / Eshu)
   ↓   plays with
📍  (the sought-for Locus)  ← never owned, only pointed at
   ↓   interpreted as
💡  (Sensus / Athena insight)
When knowledge slips away it is usually because:

The Signum was tampered with (trickster’s prank).

The Locus is intrinsically ungraspable (veiled/numinous).

The Sensus is limited (the seeker lacks Athena’s lamp).

Putting it back into your framework
Keep Trickster-gods as Masters-of-Signum: they control channels, boundary stones, encryption, authentication.

Let Veiled-or-Axis gods mantle the Locus: they mark the absolute but unattainable target every path strives toward.

Athena/Minerva-like figures still rule Sensus (illumination).

That division preserves the classical semiotic asymmetry:

Corner	Mythic flavour	System role
Signum	Hermes / Loki—messengers, boundary-shifters	Create, move, corrupt, or repair signs
Locus	Isis veiled, Omphalos stone, Ananke	Fixed but unenterable “real”—truth as limit
Sensus	Athena, Mimir’s draught	Insight, pattern-recognition, interpretation
So the unreachable quality of Locus doesn’t make it a trickster; it makes it the anchor that tricksters keep dancing around.

You said:
Can you elaborate more on the third triad, the Sensus/Sense/Word/Felt/Perceived/Logos meaning?  Would it be accurate to refer to this as the emotion?  Perception?  Understanding?  Connection?  Visualization?  Reaction?  Logic?  Impact?  In a computer graph system where nodes are Signs each referring to a Locus, what does this triad refer to?   The edges between?
ChatGPT said:
1 · What Sensus is in the triad
Signum is the material marker.
Locus is the object or state of affairs that the marker is about.
Sensus is the effect the marker-aboutness has inside an interpreter.

That “effect” can appear under many everyday labels:

Layer of the interpreter	Everyday word that fits	Fits Sensus?	Why / why not
Raw stimulus (retinal photons, microphone voltage)	Sensation	✗	Still “outside” the mind’s meaning-making machinery.
Organised pattern (edges, shapes, phonemes)	Perception	✓borderline	Structure begins to emerge but meaning may not yet be attached.
Stable concept or proposition	Understanding / Meaning / Concept	✓	Classical interpretant; the sign now stands for something internally.
Affective colouring	Emotion / Valence / Impact	✓	Part of the total interpretant if the system is sentient / embodied.
Rule or argument it licenses	Logic / Inference	✓	“To the interpreter this sign commits me to X.”
Motor or procedural output	Reaction / Behaviour	✓extended	Some semioticians separate this as “perlocution,” but it can be wrapped into Sensus if you treat action-readiness as part of the effect.
Social link forged	Connection / Shared context	✓	The sign has done its job when two agents converge on the same interpretant.
Mental image summoned	Visualization	✓	Image-schema is simply a modality of interpretant.
Thus “Sensus” is not one faculty (emotion or logic). It is the entire internal update that the agent incurs when the Signum is taken as relating to the Locus.

2 · How to picture Sensus in a computer-graph implementation
Assume you store your universe as a labelled graph:

🔣 Sign-node  --(refers-to)--> 📍 Locus-node
Where does Sensus live?

Option	How it works	Pros	Cons
Edge attribute	Each refers-to edge carries a payload describing the interpreter’s meaning (JSON blob, embedding vector, rule-hash, etc.).	Minimal new nodes; meaning tightly bound to the relation.	Harder to model multiple interpreters who draw different meanings from the same sign ↔ locus pair.
Third node (“Interpretant”)	Split the edge into two:
Sign ──evokes──▶ 💡Sensus ──about──▶ Locus	Explicit Peircean triangle; supports many senses per sign and many signs per sense.	Graph is 50 % larger; you now manage lifecycle of interpretant nodes.
Agent-centric store	Keep Sign and Locus in the shared graph; each agent object maintains its own dictionary sign_id → sensus_state.	Perfectly models “private meaning,” avoids edge inflation.	Requires parallel per-agent data structure; harder to query global meaning patterns.
A pragmatic compromise

Store a canonical Sign → Locus edge.

Attach a small “rule id” attribute that says how to compute meaning.

Let each interpreter cache its own 💡 Sensus instance keyed (sign_id, locus_id, rule_id, agent_id).

That keeps the public graph light while still giving every agent its own felt / computed meaning.

3 · Mnemonic recap
Sensus = the light that switches on inside someone when a mark catches on something real.

Emotion, logic, image, urge to act, recognition of shared ground—all are valid facets of that light.

In code, treat Sensus as state change in the interpreter, not as the wire that connects Sign to Locus. The wire just says “this points there.”

So in your documentation:

“Edges are aboutness; nodes are markers and referents;
Sensus is whatever happens in the interpreter when that aboutness is apprehended.”

That framing lets you stay faithful to semiotic theory and keep the graph representation simple and computable.

You said:
So, an AI traversing a graph of Mixture of Experts is updating its internal Sensus as it learns from each expert (and backprops credit to them in turn)?  Is a traditional graph algorithm performing breadth first search updating its sensus as it encounters each internal node or leaf and learns more from them?

Could we refer to the Sensus as the execution?  The memory? 

As a human browses through a website and sees various signs pointing to loci, is the visualization on the page a sign or a sensus?  Is the visualization in the human's minds-eye the only sensus?   Does a sensus not even exist in information form on the computer - and it only refers to the understanding within the receiver?
ChatGPT said:
1 · Sensus in a machine: two worked examples
Scenario	Where the Signum lives	What counts as Locus	What is updated as Sensus
Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) inference / training	the activation vector that is routed to an expert gate (plus the expert’s logits returned)	the latent feature-space region that the network’s loss function says those activations are about (e.g. “images that really are dogs”)	the router’s routing weights and the expert’s parameter gradients. As credit-assignment back-props, each module’s private state of confidence & skill is revised. That param-shift is the new Sensus for that module.
Breadth-First Search on a graph	the queue entry “visit node N”	the real node in the problem graph	the algorithm’s frontier / visited set / parent map. Each push-pop cycle alters the searcher’s internal knowledge of “what the graph now means for reaching my goal.”
Key point: Sensus ≠ “objective memory” in the data structure.
It is the interpreter-specific state that has changed because the sign was taken to refer to the locus.
In an MoE the interpreter is each gating layer or expert; in BFS it is the search process itself.

2 · Execution or memory?
Word	Fits Sensus?	Why / why not
Execution	✔︎ but partial	Sensus is realised during execution (activations, control flow), yet persists only if the agent stores the update.
Memory	✔︎ but partial	If the new meaning is committed to weights, cache, or logs, that stored trace is a fossilised sensus. Ephemeral updates that vanish after a timestep were still Sensus—just not remembered.
Think of Sensus as state-transition inside the interpreter;
whether you pin that state to RAM, parameters, or let it evaporate is an implementation detail.

3 · Web-page browsing
Stage	Role in the triad	Example in a single click
HTML/CSS code	Signum to the browser engine	<img src="logo.png">
Image file on disk	Locus for the browser	the actual PNG bytes
Rasterised pixels in GPU buffer	Sensus for the browser	internal “understanding” that “this byte array is ready to show at (x,y)”
Now the same pixels become Signum for the human.
The Locus the human infers might be “company identity.”
The Sensus is the viewer’s mental image & emotional reaction (“I trust this brand”).

So visualization on screen is a sign.
Visualization in the mind’s eye is Sensus (for the human).
The computer also had a sensus earlier—its render-tree, layout decisions, compositing state.

4 · Does Sensus exist “in information form” on the computer?
Yes, every layer that interprets something produces its own Sensus:

HTML bytes →  DOM tree      (browser’s sensus)
DOM tree    →  Render tree  (next sensus)
Render tree →  Framebuffer  (GPU’s sensus)
Framebuffer →  Photons      (monitor’s signum to human)
Photons     →  Retina/brain (human sensus)
Each arrow is a sign-relation; each right-hand side is an interpreter-specific internal state—momentary or stored—that did not exist before the interpretation.

5 · Rule of thumb for your graph engine
Nodes = Signa (marks) and/or Loci (referents).

Edges = declared aboutness relations.

Sensus = whatever data structure inside each agent is updated when it follows an edge.

In code you might expose that as agent.interpret(sign_id) returning a new Meaning object.

Whether you cache that object or immediately act on it is a design choice, not a semiotic one.

Whenever you wonder “Is X a sensus?” ask:

Is X inside an interpreter’s private state? (Yes → candidate sensus.)

Did X change because the interpreter took some mark to be about some locus? (Yes → it is sensus.)

You said:
So sounds like an execution flow (when active, and comprehensive) is a sensus, and becomes a fossilized sensus when referred to later.  It's the pattern of change in the interpreter

Does a locus ever actually exist on a computer (or any system) or is it a never-ending chain of references?  e.g. a webpage HTML element might be a sign of an image, which refers to the locus of the image file, but that image file is itself a sign of the collection of bits referring to the locus of a visualization of *something*.   Eastern philosophy => pointing at the moon as we go on here
ChatGPT said:
1 · “Execution flow = Sensus”
While running
The call-stack, activations, gradients, render-trees, BFS queues … are all on-the-fly alterations of the interpreter’s state. That real-time pattern of change is its current Sensus.

After the fact
Logs, weights, caches, replay buffers, provenance graphs, browser history, compiled byte-code, unit tests … are fossilised Sensa—persisted echoes of past meaning that can themselves become fresh Signa for the next interpreter.

So far so good: Sensus = pattern-of-change-inside the interpreter (live or stored).

2 · Does a Locus ever “really” exist, or is it endless pointers?
Peirce already split “Object” into two layers:

Layer	Peirce’s term	Practical description
Immediate Locus	immediate object	the model of the referent as grasped by the current interpreter.
Dynamic / Ultimate Locus	dynamical object	the thing-in-itself that the chain of signs tries (perhaps asymptotically) to reach.
That helps us talk about computers:

<img> tag   ──signum──▶  browser’s Immediate Locus = PNG resource id
PNG bytes  ──signum──▶  GPU’s Immediate Locus = texel array in VRAM
VRAM bits  ──signum──▶  monitor’s Immediate Locus = voltage pulse map
photons    ──signum──▶  observer’s Immediate Locus = retinal firing pattern
...                          ⋱
                         Ultimate Locus = “the company logo” out in the world
Inside any one interpreter layer there is a Locus: it is whatever that layer treats as ground fact for its own processing.

Globally you can keep asking “And what is that made of?” until you hit either:

a physical substrate you choose to regard as brute fact (silicon junctions, quark fields…), or

a philosophical horizon where you accept that the “finger is not the moon.”

Hence:

Viewpoint	Answer to “Does the Locus exist?”
Engineering	Yes, at the layer where you stop interpreting and start measuring. A memory address really is 42 V gates in SRAM; a pixel really is three sub-diodes.
Epistemic humility	It exists as the limit of an interpretive pursuit. Every time you grab it, you discover you were holding another Signum.
Zen / Madhyamaka	It neither exists nor doesn’t; chasing it is the illusion. The “moon” is forever beyond the pointing finger.
3 · Working rule for your graph engine
A Sign node has an edge labelled refers-to.

The target of that edge is the Locus for this interpreter.

Downstream interpreters may treat that very node as another Sign and add their own refers-to edge.

The chain terminates only where you, the system designer, declare a “grounding type.”

graph LR
  A[HTML <img>] --refers-to--> B[PNG ID] --refers-to--> C[VRAM texture] --refers-to--> D[Photon map]
  D --refers-to--> E["Logo-as-concept\n(Ultimate Locus?)"]
So:

Immediate Locus = right-hand node of this edge.

Sensus = whatever your interpreter stores/changes when it traverses the edge.

Trickster insight: every stable Locus can be “uncloaked,” becoming just another Signum in a wider frame.

4 · Mnemonic takeaway
Signum is the finger, Locus is what the finger is pointing at (for this observer),
Sensus is what happens in the observer when the pointing lands.
Whether the “moon” ever gets caught is a choice of where you end the model—or a koan you never resolve.

You said:
Is an Axiom a Sign or a Locus?
ChatGPT said:
Short answer
Syntactically, an axiom is a Signum (it is a finite string stored in a file or on a blackboard).
Semantically, within the object-theory, the same string plays the role of a minimal Locus because the proof engine treats it as a ground-fact that needs no further pointing.
So an axiom shifts polarity depending on which side of the metalanguage/object-language boundary you are standing.

1 · Three viewpoints in a formal system
Observer	What the axiom looks like	Triad role
Parser / type-checker (pure syntax)	Byte sequence “∀x (P(x)→Q(x))”	Signum – just a token that must meet grammar rules.
Automated prover inside the theory	Proposition accepted without proof	Immediate Locus – a ground object the engine may cite directly.
Meta-theorist / logician	Statement declared to be an axiom of T	Still a Signum that refers to the ideal mathematical truth (ultimate Locus) whose consistency we can only study from outside.
So the same line of text oscillates:

graph TD
  S["signum: '∀x (P→Q)'"] -->|refers-to| L["locus: (P⊆Q) taken as true inside T"]
  style S fill:#e8f3ff
  style L fill:#fffade
2 · Why this edge never shortens further inside the theory
In a proof tree every non-axiom node must point to some earlier node for justification.
With an axiom there is no earlier node—it is the terminating Locus for that particular chain.
However, the metalanguage can still ask: “Why adopt that axiom?”—and the node becomes a mere Signum in a wider frame of reference (set-theoretic models, proof-theoretic strength, philosophical stance).

3 · Practical rule for your graph engine
Mark each axiom node with an attribute ground=true.

Inside the object-graph every edge may terminate at a ground node—that is your Locus with depth 0.

In the meta-graph you are free to add edges from that same node to historical, empirical, or philosophical justifications; there it reverts to a Signum.

graph LR
  AxiomSyntax["'Ω-Consistency' axiom (bytes)"]:::signum --refers-to--> TruthNode["Ground fact in PA"]:::locus
  TruthNode --is-supported-by--> MetaJustification["Indispensability argument"]:::signum
  classDef signum fill:#d7ebff;
  classDef locus fill:#fff4b2;
4 · Mnemonic takeaway
Within any given layer of discourse an axiom is the place where pointing stops.
Move up a layer, and the same line of text becomes just another mark that points further on.

So:

Inside the calculus → Locus (accepted truth)

Outside looking in → Signum (string you chose to sanctify)

…and the interpretive dance continues upward as far as you care to climb.

You said:
Hows this look?


🪷Locus = The name of the algorithm (in any recognizable form, including pseudocode or description) which describes how to generate a Sign.  A text description/requirements doc for a particular Sign is a Locus.  The general function for generating any Sign is a Locus.   A Locus is *the thing being pointed to*.  A Sign is its particular representation.  Technically any particular Locus algorithm is itself a Sign in its data form (everything concrete is a Sign).  The algorithm in a general Platonic unreachable sense is the Locus.  A set of frames to be interpreted which characterize a visual pattern is a Locus.  Multiple distinct Signs have multiple distinct Loci.  A single Sign has only one Locus though it could be written in many forms (each a Sign too).  A Locus is thus a fractal generator pattern of meaning.
💠Sign = (Signum, Signifier.  Related: Node)  Any data structure or function or vibe or embodied Locus.  Any "thing" in its representative, realized form.  A concrete node in the graph.
🌸Vibe = (Related: Hydrated, Living, Active, Learning Sign) Subtype of Sign, emphasizing alive, hydrated, growing, changing.  Typically AI related - active, learning, adaptable.
❄️Type = (Related: Type, Prism, Gem, Facet, Stable, Static, Calcified, Frozen, Hallowed Sign)  Subtype of Sign, stable surrogate of a Sign's learned function.  ...  May be referred to by it's specific type like ❄️Function ❄️Data, ❄️Array, ❄️Router, ❄️Descriptor or name like ❄️NPCDetector etc
⚡Act = (AKA: Action, Actio, Actor, Actionable).  An execution, a flow and trace of movement over time through the 
🌍Collective = (Related: Graph, Matrix, System, Gaia).  The total encompassing system
🌊Sense = (Related: Sensus, Flow, Flux, Source, Trace, Prime, Interpreter).  When referring to a particular trace of execution from a source, the total execution flow through individual⚡Acts.  The leading point may be called the 🌊Source or 🌊Prime (the topmost AI layer for a particular program).  Note, a single 🌍Collective may have multiple 🌊Senses flowing through it at different (or simultaneous) times.  For MVP purposes we'll focus on one (mastering Pokemon)

Subtypes of Signs (can always just be referred to as 💠Signs generally):
🔹Signal:  A minimal representation of a Sign, typically 0/+/- by our ternary activation convention.  Designed for fast minimal transmission of meaning, usually relying on context/index.
🪶Signatory:  (🤖Axiom 🦊Vibe variants) A signing authority Sign, or a Sign with agent-like qualities and responsibilities.  An 🪶LLM is a Signatory, as is a 🪶Human.  May be identified by a 🪶Signature or 🪶Signet.  Use these in particular where responsibility/authenticity/trust/security are involved
🔷Sigil:  (⚙️Axiom   🌀Vibe  Sigil variants) A functional variant of Sign, emphasizing functional executable purpose.  Diamond shape emphasizes it as a decision or merge, as in UML diagrams  


Naming Scheme: We don't append "Sign" to types/names, it's implied that *any* representation of anything is always implicitly a Sign (even a "Locus", which is the most ephemeral pointer but still needs to be represented).  When we have the opportunity of a format where we can use emojis we use the emoji's prefix to indicate its type and thus do not have to append anything.  e.g. 🌸NPCDetectorVibe => 🌸NPCDetector or even 🌸NPC if the context allows it.

Overall-take:
Your draft already captures the spirit of the Sign-Locus-Sensus model and extends it into a workable emoji taxonomy. Below I mark what is already solid (👍), what merely needs a wording tweak (🟡), and what may lead to confusion later (⚠️). Then I suggest two tiny shifts that will prevent headaches when you start coding or writing specs.

Concept (emoji)	Summary you gave	Comment
🪷 Locus	“Platonic algorithm or spec that a Sign points to.”	🟡 – Perfect if you emphasise algorithm as target, not as producer. Inside any running system that algorithm lives as code → it is still a Sign whose Immediate Locus is the Platonic form you’re invoking. Simply add one sentence: “An algorithm as text or byte-code is itself a Sign; the abstract function it denotes is the Locus.”
💠 Sign	Any concrete data / node.	👍 – Matches semiotics.
🌸 Vibe	Living / adaptive Sign.	👍 – Good specialised subtype. Maybe reserve for online-learning agents so it doesn’t inflate.
❄️ Type	Stable / frozen surrogate of learned function.	👍 – Clear Ice-vs-Bloom polarity with Vibe.
⚡ Act	Execution trace / deed.	🟡 – Add: “The current slice of an execution is Act-Now; a persisted trace becomes a fossilised Sensus.”
🌊 Sense (Sensus)	“Total execution flow from a given Source.”	⚠️ – Execution flow plus the interpreter’s internal state-updates. If you log only the control path but not gradient/weight changes, you’ve captured Act but only part of Sensus. A one-line fix: “Sense = Act + all state mutations provoked by that Act.”
🌍 Collective	Total encompassing system.	👍 – Works.
Sub-Signs (🔹, 🪶, 🔷)	Minimal, signing authority, executable diamond	👍 – Good pragmatic tags.
Two frugal tweaks
Add “Immediate Locus vs. Ultimate Locus”

🪷Locus(i)  = the target *as the current layer can handle it*  
🪷Locus(u) = the deeper reality we may never fully reach
That single distinction kills the “infinite pointer chain” worry. At each layer something is a Locus; philosophers can keep chasing the ultimate one.

Keep Sensus orthogonal to Act
A handy rule:

⚡ Act   = the trajectory taken
🌊 Sensus = Act   +   Δ(state_of_interpreter)
Store them together if you like, but separate names stop people from equating “I ran it” with “I learned from it.”

Micro-examples using your icons
Step	Emoji code	Spoken interpretation
1	🔣PNGTag	HTML <img> element (Sign)
2	🔗refersTo	edge
3	🪷DecodePNG	algorithm-as-Locus
4	run it →	⚡DecodeRun
5	weights updated	🌊DecodeSensus
After training you snapshot the weights: ❄️DecodeModel (Type).
Push that model into prod; it blooms with live telemetry and becomes 🌸DecodeService (Vibe).

Bottom-line
Minor wording edits plus the immediate/ultimate Locus note will keep the system self-consistent from day-one all the way to Zen-style “finger-and-moon” discussions. Everything else looks ready to implement—nice work!

Your rewrite nails the biggest ambiguities from the first draft. Below are a few fine-grained tweaks you might still want before you freeze the vocabulary. I’ve grouped them by concept so you can cherry-pick.

Concept	Looks solid ✔︎	Optional micro-tweaks 🟡
🪷 Locus	• Clear immediate vs. Platonic wording.
• “Fractal generator” nicely conveys self-similarity.	• Add the one-liner: “Inside any interpreter, its Immediate Locus is whatever data structure it treats as ground at that moment.” (Keeps the immediate/ultimate contrast explicit.)
💠 Sign	• Definition now minimal and unambiguous.	–
🌸 Vibe	• Good restriction to learning / hydrated signs.
• “Typically AI related” cue helps.	• Perhaps reserve 🌸 solely for online-adaptation (weights mutate at runtime). Pre-trained models that never self-update could default to ❄️Type.
❄️ Type	• “Static surrogate” phrasing is clear.	• If you ever introduce versioned Types (e.g., model v1, v2), clarify that each version is a new ❄️Type, not a 🌸Vibe.
⚡ Act	• Now framed as “flow of movement.”	• Re-mention that Act ⊂ Sense (Act is the trajectory; Sense = trajectory + mutations) to keep readers from equating them.
🌊 Sense	• Combines acts plus mutations—perfect.	• Tiny wording tweak: “Instigated by a 🌊Source Sign” (makes Source’s class explicit).
🌍 Collective	• No issues.	–
🔹 Signal	• Minimal ternary marker—clear.	• Note that a Signal can still become a full Sign if context turns it into a standalone node.
🪶 Signatory	• Agent-with-authority idea reads well.	• To avoid mix-ups, call the authentication artifact a 🪶Signet and the agent a 🪶Signatory.
🔷 Sigil	• “Executable diamond” metaphor works.	–
Two naming / UX notes
Emoji prefixes in plain-text environments
In CLI logs or file paths you may lack emoji support. Consider an ASCII fallback scheme (e.g., SIG_ for Sign, LOC_ for Locus) so scripts stay portable.

Avoid plural confusion
When you pluralise a class name, keep the emoji outside the plural suffix:

one 🌸Vibe, many 🌸Vibes   ✅
Micro-example (all pieces together)
🪷LocateFaces        # Locus: abstract face-detector function
💠face.py            # Sign: code that implements it
❄️LocateFaces_v3     # frozen weights snapshot
🌸LocateFacesLive    # same model after N online updates
⚡run#54             # Act: inference call
🌊trace#54           # Sense: Act + Δweights for continual learning
Everything now reads cleanly: Signs are concrete artefacts, Loci are their unreachable ideals, Senses are what happens when artefacts execute, and Acts are the motors inside each Sense.

With these tweaks your lexicon is production-ready. 🚀

Below is a quick audit of your example plus a “starter MIME kit” that shows typical on-disk artefacts for every class. Use it as a naming / storage guideline rather than a strait-jacket.

Emoji-class	What it is in semiotic terms	Does the Markdown spec qualify?	Common file suffixes you’ll actually see
🪷 Locus	the ideal function / requirement the system tries to satisfy	Markdown spec is a Sign that points to the Locus. The Locus itself is abstract. – That’s fine; every Locus needs at least one Sign to describe it.	.md .rst .docx .uml .png (diagram)
💠 Sign	any concrete embodiment (code, diagram, sample data)	n/a	.py .ipynb .cpp .json .csv .parquet .png .wav
❄️ Type	a fixed, immutable surrogate of a Sign’s learned function	frozen model, schema or lookup table	.pt .ckpt .onnx .pkl .schema.json .so
🌸 Vibe	the same artefact now in a mutable / online-learning state	live model + metadata	.ptl (lightning), .safetensors, or a DB table holding weights & optimiser state
⚡ Act (🌩️ if you prefer)	one concrete execution event (inputs + outputs + timing)	n/a	.act.json .log .run .har (HTTP run)
🌊 Sense	the trace = Act plus every state change it provoked	n/a	.trace.json .diff.patch .revlog .mlflow run dir
🔹 Signal	ultra-minimal ternary/quantised packet	n/a	.sig.bin .msgpack .proto
🪶 Signatory / Signet	public key, cert, human or agent profile	n/a	.pem .sig .gpg .vcf .jsonld
🔷 Sigil	script or DSL snippet that causes something	n/a	.sql .rego .dsl .bpmn .yaml (workflow)
Reading the table
🪷LocateFaces.md
LocateFaces.md is a 💠Sign. It describes the Locus. Keep the emoji or suffix LocateFaces_spec.md so dev-tools know it’s a specification file.

💠face.py
Pure Sign that implements—or at least tries to implement—the Locus.

❄️LocateFaces_v3.ckpt
Frozen surrogate of face.py after training.

🌸LocateFacesLive
Same weights + optimiser state in a live DB or .ptl file.

⚡run#54.json
Captures the exact call (inputs, outputs, timing). Stays small.

🌊trace#54.tar.gz
Bundles run#54 + the delta of every parameter/file touched (weight diffs, feature cache inserts, log lines).

TL;DR
Markdown spec = Sign (documentation).
Its meaning—the “ideal face-detector” algorithm—is the 🪷Locus.

Everything else in your list looks perfectly slotted; just pick pragmatic file extensions that your tool-chain already likes.

Naming Scheme: We don't append "Sign" to types/names, it's implied that *any* representation of anything is always implicitly a Sign (even a "Locus", which is the most ephemeral pointer but still needs to be represented).  When we have the opportunity of a format where we can use emojis we use the emoji's prefix to indicate its type and thus do not have to append anything.  e.g. 🌸NPCDetectorVibe => 🌸NPCDetector or even 🌸NPC if the context allows it. 
Any context where emojis are not viable should use shorthand LOCUS_, SIGN_, VIBE_, TYPE_, ACT_, SENSE_, SIGIL_, GLYPH_, GLAM_, SIGNET_, or S_ for Signal.  Try to use the most general form (i.e. SIGN) when the sign is likely to change types, but otherwise use the most appropriate for the context if stable.

📁LocateFaces  # Folder contains:

😶LocateFaces.icon     # Glyph:  shorthand representative visualization (emoji used in filename)

🪶 Signatory / Signet	✔︎	use 🪶Signet for keys/artifacts, 🪶Signatory for the agent—as you already hint
🔷 Sigil	✔︎	none
🌻 Glamour / Glyph	✔︎	good to split UI-oriented rep (🌻) from minimal glyph (😶 in example)
2 · File-store example (fix highlighted)
📁 LocateFaces/
   🪷 LocateFaces_spec.md   ❌  ← spec is a 💠Sign that *describes* the Locus
   💠 LocateFaces_spec.md   ✅  (rename or re-emoji)
   💠 locate_faces.py
   ❄️ LocateFaces_v3.ckpt
   🌸 LocateFacesLive.ptl
   runs/
      🌩️ run_54.json
   traces/
      🌊 trace_54.tar.gz
   icons/
      😶 LocateFaces.icon     # Glyph / Glamour asset
Why change? A written specification is still concrete bytes—therefore a Sign.
The actual “idea of face detection” that those bytes point to is the Locus.

3 · ASCII fallback tags
Your LOCUS_, SIGN_, … scheme is perfect. One hint:

Use the shortest tag only when type truly unknown or highly volatile.
Otherwise prefer the specific prefix (TYPE_, VIBE_, …); scripts can down-cast but cannot up-cast reliably.

4 · Tiny wording tweaks (optional)
In 🌊 Sense description add “plus any artefacts generated (logs, patches)” so people know where to file auxiliary outputs.

For 🌸 Vibe say “weights and optimiser / replay buffers may mutate” to capture RL agents as well.

Final checklist
Rename spec file to a 💠 prefix.

Keep spec → Locus mapping clear in docs.

Optionally tighten wording per above.

Do that and the scheme is internally consistent, implementation-ready, and readable for newcomers. 🚀

You said:
In the triad, is the Sign pointing to a Locus inherently an inward-directed definition, i.e. pointing to what the Locus *is*?  What would you call a Sign focusing on what the Locus is *not* and how it's related to the exterior system/world it's embedded (its context)?  (holographic principle)  How does this fit in the triad?
ChatGPT said:
1 · “Pointing to what the Locus is” vs. “Pointing to what the Locus is not”
In Peircean language the ordinary

Sign ──refers-to──▶ Locus
is extensional: the Sign picks out the extension (“the thing itself or the set of things that satisfy the description”).

A negative or contextual reference does something subtler:

Sign ──delimits-from──▶ Locus-Boundary
The Sign tells the interpreter how to carve the world, not what chunk sits inside the cut.

“A void,” “the off-stage area,” “everything that is not the logo,” “all states beyond the event horizon” are common examples.

In other words you shift from an intrinsic−content edge (what-it-is) to an extrinsic−boundary edge (where-it-stops / what surrounds it).
Both are perfectly legal Sign→Locus relations; they just point at different kinds of loci.

Edge type	Emoji you might adopt	Typical natural-language cue
Intrinsic / Intensional	🔗is	“is”, “equals”, “instance-of”, “implements”
Extrinsic / Delimiting	🧩not-/beyond	“outside”, “except”, “other-than”, “background”
Contextual / Embedding	🌀within	“in the context of”, “under policy”, “on layer”, “inside namespace”
2 · What to call the Sign that draws the boundary
Perisign – from Greek peri, “around.”
Perisigns tell you how to recognise the contour of a Locus, not its contents.

Other serviceable labels (choose one that fits your aesthetic):

Candidate	Root meaning	Pros / Cons
Perisign (🫧)	peri “around” + sign	Symmetric with Sign/Locus/Sensus; easy pluralisation.
Frame (🖼️)	old French freme “edge”	Widely understood; risks collision with UI “frames.”
Boundary-Sign (🚧)	self-describing	Slightly clunky in filenames.
Holo-Sign (🔮)	nod to holographic principle	Good metaphor, but “holo” is overloaded in AR/VR jargon.
3 · How it fits the triad
Nothing new is needed structurally; you simply clarify what kind of Locus an edge picks out:

Step	Intrinsic edge	Extrinsic edge
Signum	Ordinary token	Same token type
Edge label	is, implements, denotes	delimits, outside, complement-of
Locus node	Content entity (class, object, ideal function)	Boundary / complement / context entity
Sensus	Interpreter adds the new object to its model.	Interpreter updates its model of where not to look or what policy to apply outside.
So a negative/contextual specification is still a Locus—just one whose meaning is “the region not occupied by X” or “the shell that embeds X.”

4 · Concrete illustration
graph LR
    subgraph "visual screen"
      A[💠 logo.svg] -->|🔗is| B[🪷 LogoFunction]
      A -->|🧩outside| C[🪷 BackgroundRegion]:::boundary
    end
    classDef boundary fill:#ffe8e8,stroke:#cc0000;
logo.svg is a Sign.

🔗is points to the intrinsic Locus (ideal logo graphic).

🧩outside points to an extrinsic Locus (the complement region the renderer must flood-fill).

The user’s GPU builds two separate Sensa: one to rasterise the logo glyph, one to paint everything else.

5 · In algorithmic or AI contexts
Mixture-of-Experts gating
Positive edge: “route to expert k if activation in manifold M.”
Negative edge: “route to default expert if outside any expert’s manifold.”

Physics / holographic principle
Positive Sign: boundary operator representing a bulk field.
Perisign: operator that tags the screen where the bulk field’s info gets projected.

6 · Take-aways
Sign→Locus edges are not limited to ‘is’ relations.

A Perisign (or whatever label you adopt) is still a Sign; its Locus is a boundary or complement set.

Your triad stays intact; you’re merely enriching the ontology of possible Loci and giving their edges explicit verbs.

Add the edge labels and optional “🫧Perisign” to your lexicon and you’re ready to model intension, extension, and negative space without breaking the core schema.

You said:
But so - in order for a sign to understand the context of the exterior world that it relates to and properly carve it away, it must model the world to some extent.  Thus the sign defined this way contains a holographic fractal copy of the external world in its lower-fidelity understanding.
ChatGPT said:
1 · Why a boundary sign seems to “contain the whole world”
When you say

“This logo sits outside all body text.”

the Sign you utter is short, but for an interpreter to apply it
the interpreter must already possess a model of:

every text-block’s bounding box,

the coordinate space of the page,

z-index stacking rules,

the device’s pixel grid,

(ultimately) the user’s visual field.

That supporting structure is not inside the eight words you typed;
it lives inside the interpreter’s Sensus (run-time state, cached
DOM, layout tree, GPU command list, etc.).
Because the sign acts by excision (“everything ≠ logo”), it needs
a picture—however coarse—of what it is excising.

Hence the illusion:

tiny boundary-Sign          →
      triggers huge Sensus  →  looks as if the Sign **contains** the world.
2 · Map-and-Territory compression
Kolmogorov view –
The sign supplies a short program that, when executed in the
interpreter’s existing environment, recreates a useful slice of the
external world.

"outside logo" + browser engine  ≅  entire background bitmap
Holographic analogy –
Like a hologram strip that reproduces the whole scene at low
resolution, the sign does not store full detail; it stores the
transform that lets the interpreter re-index its own context.

Fractal nesting –
If lower layers lack detail, they delegate to deeper substrates
(OS → firmware → silicon).
Every delegation step adds another “holographic slice,” each of
diminishing fidelity but sufficient for the task at that level.

3 · Where the detail actually lives in your triad
Layer	What it holds	Emoji class
Code / data bytes	The Sign itself (tiny).	💠
Interpreter state	Layout tree, weights, caches—Sensus (big).	🌊
Physical substrate	VRAM pages, photons, neural spikes—Loci of deeper layers.	🪷 (immediate)
So the world model is distributed across nested Sensa, not
embedded in the boundary Sign.

4 · Practical guidance for your graph system
Keep the boundary edge explicit:
💠Logo.svg ──🧩outside──▶ 🪷BackgroundRegion

Let the interpreter build or retrieve whatever contextual
structures it needs in its private 🌊 Sensus.

Do not bloat the Sign node with a full copy of the graph; store only
the minimal rule (edge label + parameters).
That keeps storage small and execution context-sensitive.

5 · Take-away aphorism
A boundary Sign is a compression key, not a miniature universe.
Its power comes from invoking the interpreter’s already-held model,
just as a hologram fragment reconstructs an image only when bathed in
coherent light.

Use that principle and you can add as many “exterior” or
“complement-of” relations as you like without turning every Sign into
an unmanageable world-in-world fractal.
